(Algebraic) p p -adic Artin formalism of twisted triple product Galois representations over real quadratic fields
Bhargab Das, Aprameyo Pal

Abstract
. In this article, we investigate factorization problems for twisted triple product Galois representations over real quadratic fields, arising from families of Hilbert cusp forms. Specifically, we address the factorization in two distinct settings determined by the order of vanishing of associated L L -functions at their central critical values-namely, the rank (1,1) and rank (0,2) cases. Our results generalize the algebraic factorization framework developed by Büyükboduk et al. in higher rank scenario to the setting of real quadratic fields. Notably, our work yields the first known factorization result in the higher rank (0,2) case, marking a significant advancement in the study of triple product motives over totally real fields.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11R23, Secondary 11F41, 11G40, 14G10

1.
Introduction

The foremost purpose of this article is to study the factorization problem for certain twisted triple product (algebraic)
p
-adic
L
-functions. We explore this problem in an irregular setup in the sense of Perrin-Riou’s
p
-adic
L
-functions and
‘regular’
submodule. Our work is an real quadratic extension of the results obtained in
[
BS25
]
in two cases namely rank (1,1) (cf.
[
DR16
, §4.5.2]
) and rank (0,2) (cf.
[
DR16
, §4.5.3]
). Additionally we highlight significant differences/distinctions between our problem and those in
[
Gre82
,
Pal18
]
in §
1.1
, which makes our cases particularly challenging.

Let us fix a prime
p\geq 5
once and for all. We also choose
p
-adic embedding
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
, and a complex embedding
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
. We also fix an isomorphism
\iota_{p}\colon\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
, such that the following diagram commutes with our choices for a fixed real quadratic
F/\mathbb{Q}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{p}}

We also assume that
p
splits in
F
and set
\operatorname{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})=\{1,\theta\}
. Let
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{g}
be cuspidal primitive Hida families of Hilbert modular forms (see
Section

2
for more details) and
\mathbf{g}^{\theta}
is the
\theta
-twist of
\mathbf{g}
. Suppose
\mathbf{g}^{\theta}\cong\mathbf{g}\otimes\omega
for a quadratic character
\omega
. The first task we undertook is to formulate precise conjectures about factorization in the following cases:

(1,1)

The
(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})
-unbalanced
p
-adic triple product
L
-functions
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))
in
Conjecture

2
which predicts that

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))(P,Q)=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{ad}^{0}{\mathbf{g}}(\lambda))(P,Q)\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{\rm LZ})\right)

for a quadratic Galois character
\lambda
related to
\omega
. Here the factor
\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{\rm LZ})
is related to the Euler system constructed in
[
LZ20a
]
which is closely associated to the derivative of
p
-adic
L
-function of
\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda
(see §
5.1.2
).

(0,2)

The
\mathbf{g}
-unbalanced
p
-adic triple product
L
-functions
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))
in
Conjecture

3
predicting that

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathrm{bal}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{ad}^{0}{\mathbf{g}}(\lambda))\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}^{2}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2})\right),

for a quadratic Galois character
\lambda
related to
\omega
. Here we consider the conjectural existence of rank 2 Euler system
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2}
by
(Plec)
which has evidence in some recent works of Henri Darmon, Michele Fornea, Lennart Gehrmann and many more (cf.
[
FG23
,
DF25
]
, also see §
6.1.2
).

In both cases we consider the situation when the interpolation range is empty and sign conditions such that analogues problem for
\mathbf{f}
-dominant and balanced triple product
p
-adic
L
-functions reduces to
0=0
.

The general theory of Iwasawa-Greenberg main conjecture would suggest an algebraic counterpart of these two cases of factorization conjectures. The author gave some evidence towards these versions of the main conjectures in
[
Das24
]
. These (algebraic factorizations) are formulated in terms of
“modules of leading terms”
. Inspired from
[
BS25
]
we defined and study these objects in §
4
in a much general context and produced some useful results for
“rank 2”
scenario, using the general theory of exterior power by dual modules. The results we got are as follows:

(1,1)

The module of
(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})
-unbalanced (algebraic)
p
-adic triple product
L
-functions
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\rm alg}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}),\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})
can be written as

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\rm alg}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}),\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\rm alg}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{ad}^{0}{\mathbf{g}}(\lambda),\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{\rm LZ})\right),

for a quadratic Galois character
\lambda
related to
\omega
. We urge the reader to check
Theorem

A
and
Theorem

5.14
for this result written in the modules of leading terms language.

(0,2)

The module of
\mathbf{g}
-unbalanced (algebraic)
p
-adic triple product
L
-functions
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\rm alg}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}),\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
factors as

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\rm alg}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}),\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\rm alg}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{ad}^{0}{\mathbf{g}}(\lambda),\tr\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}})\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}^{2}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2})\right),

for a quadratic Galois character
\lambda
related to
\omega
. Again we request the reader to see
Theorem

B
and
Theorem

6.12
for the modules of leading terms version.

We also remark that these are unlike the scenario considered in
[
Pal18
]
(see §
1.1
for more discussion) and reflect of the
irregularity
of the factorization problems discussed in this paper. We also state the difference between our work and that in the algebraic part of
[
BS25
]
in terms of complications arising from Greenberg-local conditions (see §
1.1
and §
3.1
for more discussion on Greenberg-local conditions).

1.1.
Comparison with earlier work

We explain in this subsection about a comparison of the setting of our present work with that of
[
Gre82
,
Pal18
,
BS25
]
and highlight the key technical differences and difficulties.

1.1.1.

We have considered the representations over
G_{F}
instead of
G{Q}
for a real quadratic field
F/\mathbb{Q}
. In order to do so we have to consider local conditions in two different places associated to
\mathfrak{p}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{2}
separately along with the bad places. This is a stark contrast between our work and results in
[
Gre82
,
Pal18
,
BS25
]
as in these previous works the authors only considered representations of
G{Q}
.

In fact we hope that we can further pursue our factorization results in more
analytic
way extending the results of
[
Gro80
,
Das16
,
BCPd24
]
towards real quadratic field along with shedding some lights about BDP philosophy for Hilbert modular forms.

1.1.2.

The authors in
[
Gre82
,
Pal18
]
considered only Selmer complexes with torsion cohomology group (cf.
[
BS25
, Apendix A]
and
[
BS25
, §2.4.3]
). But the complexes in exact triangles (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) in our consideration have non-torsion cohomology groups like the situation in
[
BS25
]
. Additionally we have shown that we may have rank two cohomology groups via the Panchiskin defect (see §
3.2.2
) which is not the case of
[
BS25
]
. Thus we have extracted more complicated formulas regarding higher rank situations in §
4
accounts for the appearance of
p
-adic regulators and derivatives of
p
-adic
L
-functions in our setup. This is the first time such a result has been established for the higher rank (0,2) case, highlighting the novelty of our approach.

1.1.3.
Twisted triple product over
\mathbb{Q}

We conclude this section discussing the triple product when
\mathbf{f}
is a family of cuspforms on
\operatorname{GL}_{2,\mathbb{Q}}
and
\mathbf{g}
is as usual. Then the
8
-dimensional representation
T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g})
(cf.
[
FJ24
, Definition 6.4]
) has similar decomposition like (
2.12
). In this situation the factorization problem of (algebraic)
p
-adic
L
-functions (which are conjecturally related with the
p
-adic
L
-functions constructed in
[
BF20
]
) has almost similar flavor like the one in
[
BS25
]
with a twist by quadratic character. We will discuss this scenario more precisely in an imminent survey article about the
p
-adic Artin formalism phenomena.

1.2.
Layout

We sketch an outline of the structure of our article. We briefly introduce the basic notions about Hilbert cuspforms in §
2.1
, §
2.2
, and §
2.3
. Thereafter we define the Asai representation in §
2.4
and use this to explain our set-up in §
2.5
. Then we record our main results about
rank (1,1) case
(resp.
rank (0,2) case
) in §
2.7
(resp. §
2.8
).

In
Section

3
, we recall the machinery of Selmer complexes constructed in
[
Nek06
]
. Using this we define the module of (algebraic)
p
-adic
L
-functions of Galois representations of our concern. We make a choice of suitable Greenberg-local conditions to study the factorization problems. We also computed necessary Tamagawa factors in §
3.3
which is useful to show the perfectness of Selmer complexes.

In
Section

4
, we introduce a canonical submodule of an extended Selmer module, which is called the module of leading terms and discuss its relation with Kolyvagin systems in
Remark

4.16
.

Based on this construction, we prove Theorem
A
in
Section

5
and Theorem
B
in
Section

6
.

.

2.
The Setup

As mentioned in
Section

1
Let us fix a prime
p\geq 5
once and for all. We also choose
p
-adic embedding
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
, and a complex embedding
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
. We also fix an isomorphism
\iota_{p}\colon\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}
, such that the following diagram commutes with our choices for a fixed real quadratic
F/\mathbb{Q}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{p}}

In this section at first, we will recall some preliminary results about families of Hilbert cuspforms.

2.1.
Hilbert Cuspforms

Suppose
F
be a real quadratic number field with Galois group
\operatorname{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})=\{1,\theta\}
and ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Let us denote the set of embedding
F\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
as
I_{F}
and put
t_{F}=\sum_{\tau\in I_{F}}[\tau]\in\mathbb{Z}[I_{F}]
. We also assume that
p
splits in
\mathcal{O}_{F}
. We denote
\mathfrak{d}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}
as the discriminant of
F
and
\mathfrak{D}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}=N_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{d}_{F/\mathbb{Q}})
. Let
\theta_{F}
be the adelic character associated with
\theta
.

Definition 2.1
.

We define a power map for every
s=\sum_{\tau\in I_{F}}s_{\tau}[\tau]
as

(\cdot)^{s}\colon F\otimes{Q}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}},\quad x\otimes c\mapsto\prod_{\tau}(c\tau(x))^{s_{\tau}}.

Let
Z_{F}(1)=F^{\times}\backslash\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}/\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{p,\times}_{F}F^{\times}_{\infty,+}
, then there is a group homomorphism induced via the cyclotomic character
\chi_{\mathrm{cycl},F}
,

\check{\chi}_{\mathrm{cycl},F}\colon Z_{F}(1)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},\quad y\mapsto y_{p}^{-t_{F}}\mid y^{\infty}\mid^{-1}_{\mathbb{A}_{F}}.

Moreover, the isomorphism
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\cong(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})\times(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
gives the factorization

\check{\chi}_{\mathrm{cycl},F}=\eta_{F}\cdot\omega_{F}.

Let
i\in\mathbb{C}
be the square root of unity and consider the Poincare half-plane
\mathfrak{H}
with respect to
i
. Let us define the algebraic group
\operatorname{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{GL}_{2,F}
as
\cal G
. There is a transitive group action of
\cal G(R)^{+}\cong\prod_{I_{F}}\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
on the complex manifold
\mathfrak{H}^{I_{F}}
and
C_{\infty}^{+}\coloneqq\operatorname{Stab}_{\cal G(R)^{+}}(\mathbf{i})
where
\mathbf{i}=(i,...,i)\in\mathfrak{H}^{I_{F}}
.

For a non zero integral ideal
\mathfrak{N}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
lets define the following open compact subgroups of
\cal G(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})

•

U_{0}(\mathfrak{N})\coloneqq\biggl\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\cal G(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}})\mid c\in\mathfrak{N}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}\biggl\}
,

•

U_{1}(\mathfrak{N})\coloneqq\biggl\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in U_{0}(\mathfrak{N})\mid d-1\in\mathfrak{N}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}\biggl\}
,

•

U^{1}(\mathfrak{N})\coloneqq\biggl\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in U_{0}(\mathfrak{N})\mid a-1\in\mathfrak{N}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}\biggl\}
,

•

V(\mathfrak{N})=U_{1}(\mathfrak{N})\cap U^{1}(\mathfrak{N})
.

Let
K\leq\cal G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
be an open compact subgroup,

Definition 2.2
.

A holomorphic Hilbert cuspform of weight
(k,w)\in\mathbb{Z}[I_{F}]^{2},\,(k-2w=m\cdot t_{F},\,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0})
, level
K
is a continuous function
f\colon\cal G(\mathbb{A})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that the following properties hold,

•

f(\alpha xu)=f(x)j_{k}(u_{\infty},\mathbf{i})^{-1}
where
\alpha\in\cal G(\mathbb{Q})
,
u\in K\cdot C_{\infty}^{+}
and the automorphy factor is

j_{k}\Bigg(\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix},z\Bigg)=(ad-bc)^{w}(cz+d)^{k}

for
\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\cal G(\mathbb{R})
,
z\in\mathfrak{H}^{I_{F}}
.

•

for every finite adelic point
x\in\cal G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
the well-defined function
f_{x}\colon\mathfrak{H}^{I_{F}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
given by
f_{x}(z)=f(xu_{\infty})j_{k}(u_{\infty},\mathbf{i})
is holomorphic, where for each
z\in\mathfrak{H}^{I_{F}}
we choose
u_{\infty}\in\cal G(\mathbb{R})^{+}
such that
u_{\infty}\mathbf{i}=z
.

•

for all adelic points
x\in\cal G(\mathbb{A})
and for all additive measures on
F\backslash\mathbb{A}_{F}
we have

\int_{F\backslash\mathbb{A}_{F}}\Bigg(\begin{pmatrix}1&a\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}x\Bigg)da=0

•

for all finite adelic point
x\in\cal G(A^{\infty})
the function
\mid\Im(z)^{k/2}f_{x}(z)\mid
is uniformly bounded on
\mathfrak{H}^{I_{F}}
.

We denote the space of such Hilbert cuspforms as
S_{k,w}(K;\mathbb{C})
and when
K=U_{1}(\mathfrak{N})
it will be
S_{k,w}(\mathfrak{N};\mathbb{C})
.

Moreover,
[
Hid91
, Theorem 1.1]
states that each cuspform in
S_{k,w}(V(\mathfrak{N});\mathbb{C})
has an adelic
q
-expansion. Let
\mathbb{A}_{F,+}^{\times}\coloneqq(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})^{\times}F^{\times}_{\infty,+}
, and we denote the following

\mathsf{a}(\cdot,f)\colon\mathbb{A}_{F,+}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C},\quad\mathsf{a}_{p}(\cdot,f)\colon\mathbb{A}_{F,+}^{\times}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

as the Fourier coefficient functions for every
f\in S_{k,w}(V(\mathfrak{N});\mathbb{C})
. Then for any
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-subalgebra
A\subset\mathbb{C}
, we denote

S_{k,w}(K;A)\coloneqq\{f\in S_{k,w}(K;\mathbb{C})\mid\mathsf{a}(y,f)\in A\text{ for all }y\in\mathbb{A}_{F,+}^{\times}\}.

Remark 2.3
.

\mathsf{a}(y,f)=0=\mathsf{a}_{p}(y,f)
if
y\notin\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{\times}F^{\times}_{\infty,+}
.

2.1.1.
Hecke Theory

Let
V(\mathfrak{N})\leq K\leq U_{0}(\mathfrak{N})
be an open compact subgroup of
\cal G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
. For any
g\in\cal G(\mathbb{A})
, one can consider the following double coset operator
[KgK]
, acting on a Hilbert cuspform
f
of level
K

([KgK]f)(x)=\coprod_{i}f(x\gamma_{i})

where
\gamma_{i}
’s are coming from the decomposition
[KgK]=\coprod_{i}\gamma_{i}K
.

Definition 2.4
.

•

For every prime ideal
\mathfrak{q}
of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
and a choice of uniformizer
\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{F,\mathfrak{q}}
, the Hecke operators of
S_{k,w}(K;\mathbb{C})
are defined as

T(\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}})=\{\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}}^{w-t_{F}}\}\left[K\begin{pmatrix}\varpi&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K\right].

•

For every
a\in\mathcal{O}_{F,\mathfrak{N}}^{\times}=\prod_{\mathfrak{q}\mid\mathfrak{N}}\mathcal{O}_{F,\mathfrak{q}}^{\times}
, there is a Hecke operator

T(a,1)=\left[K\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}K\right].

•

For any
z\in Z_{\cal G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})}
in the center of
G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
, the diamond operator
\left\langle z\right\rangle
acts via

(\left\langle z\right\rangle f)(x)=f(xz).

The Hecke algebra
H_{k,w}(K;\mathcal{O}_{F})
is defined to be the
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-subalgebra of
\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{C}}\left(S_{k,w}(K;\mathbb{C})\right)
generated by the Hecke operators in the Definition
2.4
. For any
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-algebra
A
contained in
\mathbb{C}
, we have

H_{k,w}(K;A)\coloneqq H_{k,w}(K;\mathcal{O}_{F})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}A.

For every idele
y\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}\cap\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
, let
y=a\prod_{\mathfrak{q}}\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}}^{e_{\mathfrak{q}}}u
such that
u\in\det(V(\mathfrak{N}))
and
a\in\mathcal{O}_{F,\mathfrak{N}}^{\times}
. The Hecke operator

(2.1)

T_{0}(y)=T(a,1)\prod_{\mathfrak{q}}T(\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}}^{e_{\mathfrak{q}}})

depends only on
y
.

Remark 2.5
.

[
Hid91
, Theorem 2.2]

If
A
is integrally closed domain and finite flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. then
S_{k,w}(K;A)
is stable under
H_{k,w}(K;A)
and we have a nondegenerate bilinear pairing

S_{k,w}(K;A)\times H_{k,w}(K;A)\rightarrow A,\quad(f,h)\mapsto\mathsf{a}(1,h(f)).

2.2.
Hida Families of Hilbert Cuspforms

Let
O
be a valuation ring in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
finite flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
containing
\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Let
\mathfrak{N}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-ideal prime to
p
. For a open compact subgroup
K
such that
U_{1}(\mathfrak{N})\leq K\leq U_{0}(\mathfrak{N})
, we set

K(p^{r})=K\cap V(p^{r})
and
K(p^{\infty})=\cap_{r\geq 1}K(p^{r})
. Lets define the projective limit of
p
-adic Hecke algebras as

\mathbf{H}_{F}(K;O):=\varprojlim_{r}\ H_{k,w}(K(p^{r});O)

which is independent of the weight
(k,w)
. Since
\mathbf{H}_{F}(K;O)
is compact, we have a decomposition of algebras

\mathbf{H}_{F}(K;O)=\mathbf{H}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}_{F}(K;O)\oplus\mathbf{H}_{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}(K;O)

such that
\mathsf{T}(\varpi_{p})=\varprojlim_{r}T_{0}(\varpi_{p})
is a unit in
\mathbf{H}_{F}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}(K;O)
and topologically nilpotent in
\mathbf{H}_{F}^{\mathrm{ss}}(K;O)
. We denote by

(2.2)

e_{\mathrm{n.o.}}=\underset{n\to\infty}{\lim}\mathsf{T}(\varpi_{p})^{n!}

the idempotent corresponding to the nearly ordinary part
\mathbf{H}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}_{F}(K;O)
.
For any
r\geq 1
we set

(2.3)

Z^{r}_{F}(K):=\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}/F^{\times}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty}\cap\det K(p^{r}))F_{\infty,+}^{\times}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{G}_{F}^{r}(K):=Z^{r}_{F}(K)\times(\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{r}\mathcal{O}_{F})^{\times}.

If we denote the projective limits by

(2.4)

Z_{F}(K):=\varprojlim_{r}\ Z^{r}_{F}(K),\qquad\mathbb{G}_{F}(K):=\varprojlim_{r}\ \mathbb{G}^{r}_{F}(K),

then
\mathbb{G}_{F}(K)=Z_{F}(K)\times\mathcal{O}_{F,p}^{\times}
and we have a group homomorphism

(2.5)

\mathbb{G}_{F}(K)\to\mathbf{H}_{F}(K;O)^{\times},\qquad(z,a)\mapsto\langle z,a\rangle:=\left\langle z\right\rangle T(a^{-1},1)

that gives
\mathbf{H}_{F}(K;O)
a
O\llbracket\mathbb{G}_{F}(K)\rrbracket
-algebra structure.

Let
\mathrm{cl}_{F}^{+}(\mathfrak{N}p)
be the strict ray class group of modulus
\mathfrak{N}p
. We denote
\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\mathfrak{N}p}
as the closure of the totally positive units of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
congruent to
1\pmod{\mathfrak{N}p}
inside
\mathcal{O}_{F,p}^{\times}
. Hence we have an exact sequence

1\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\mathfrak{N}p}\backslash\big(1+p\mathcal{O}_{F,p}\big)\rightarrow Z_{F}(K)\rightarrow\mathrm{cl}_{F}^{+}(\mathfrak{N}p)\rightarrow 1,

which splits for large enough
p
since the order of the group
\mathrm{cl}_{F}^{+}(\mathfrak{N}p)
is prime to
p
. We denote the canonical decomposition by

(2.6)

Z_{F}(K)\overset{\sim}{\to}\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\mathfrak{N}p}\backslash\big(1+p\mathcal{O}_{F,p}\big)\times\mathrm{cl}_{F}^{+}(\mathfrak{N}p),\qquad z\mapsto\big(\xi_{z},\bar{z}\big).

If we set

\mathfrak{J}_{F}=\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{+}_{\mathfrak{N}p}\backslash\big(1+p\mathcal{O}_{F,p}\big)\times(1+p\mathcal{O}_{F,p})
, then the exact sequence

1\rightarrow\mathfrak{J}_{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{F}(K)\rightarrow\mathrm{cl}_{F}^{+}(\mathfrak{N}p)\times(\mathcal{O}_{F}/p)^{\times}\rightarrow 1

splits canonically for large enough
p
. The group
\mathfrak{J}_{F}
is a finitely generated
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-rank
[F:\mathbb{Q}]+1+\delta
, where
\delta
is Leopoldt’s defect for
F
. Let
W
be the torsion-free part of
\mathfrak{J}_{F}
and denote by

\Lambda_{F}=O\llbracket W\rrbracket
the associated completed group ring.

Theorem 2.6
.

[
Hid89b
, Theorem 2.4]

The nearly ordinary Hecke algebra
\mathbf{H}_{F}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}(K;O)
is torsion-free and finite over
\Lambda_{F}
.

The completed group ring
O\llbracket\mathbb{G}_{F}(\mathfrak{N})\rrbracket
naturally decomposes as the direct sum
\bigoplus_{\Psi}\Lambda_{F,\Psi}
ranging over all the characters of the torsion subgroup

\mathbb{G}_{F}(\mathfrak{N})_{\mathrm{tor}}=\mathfrak{I}_{F,\mathrm{tor}}\times\mathrm{cl}_{F}^{+}(\mathfrak{N}p)\times(\mathcal{O}_{F}/p)^{\times}
.
It induces a decomposition of the nearly ordinary Hecke algebra
\mathbf{H}_{F}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}(K;O)=\bigoplus_{\Psi}\mathbf{H}_{F}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}(K;O)_{\Psi}
.

Definition 2.7
.

Let
\Psi:\mathbb{G}_{F}(K)_{\mathrm{tor}}\rightarrow O^{\times}
be a character. For any
\Lambda_{F,\Psi}
-algebra
\mathcal{R}
, the space of nearly ordinary
\mathcal{R}
-adic cuspforms of tame level
K
and character
\Psi
is

\overline{\mathbf{S}}_{F}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}(K,\Psi;\mathcal{R}):=\mathrm{Hom}_{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{F,\Psi}\mbox{-}\mathrm{mod}}\big(\mathbf{H}_{F}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}(K(p^{\infty});O)_{\Psi},\mathcal{R}\big).

When an
\mathcal{R}
-adic cuspform is also a
\Lambda_{F,\Psi}
-algebra homomorphism, we call it a Hida family.

Let

\psi:\mathrm{cl}_{F}^{+}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\alpha})\rightarrow O^{\times}
,

\psi^{\prime}:(\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{\alpha})^{\times}\rightarrow O^{\times}
be a pair of characters
and
(k,w)
a weight satisfying
k-2w=mt_{F}
. The group homomorphism

\mathbb{G}_{F}(K)\to O^{\times},\qquad(z,a)\mapsto\psi(z)\psi^{\prime}(a)\check{\chi}_{\mathrm{cycl},F}(z)^{m}a^{t_{F}-w}

determines a
O
-algebra homomorphism
P_{k,w,\psi,\psi^{\prime}}:O\llbracket\mathbb{G}_{F}(K)\rrbracket\rightarrow O
.

Definition 2.8
.

For a
\Lambda_{F,\Psi}
-algebra
\mathcal{R}
the set of arithmetic points, denoted by
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{R})
, is defined as the subset of
\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{R})=\operatorname{Spf}(\mathcal{R})(\mathbb{C}_{p})
consisting of
O
-algebra homomorphisms those are equivalent with some
P_{k,w,\psi,\psi^{\prime}}
when restricted to
\Lambda_{F,\Psi}
. For any such
P\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{R})
we denote
k
as
k(P)
,
w
as
w(P)
and
m(P)\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that
k(P)-2w(P)=m(P).t_{F}
.

By the
[
Hid89b
, Theorem 2.4]
, for any such
P\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{R})
and a Hida family
\mathbf{f}\in\overline{\mathbf{S}}_{F}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}(K;\mathcal{R})
, the composite
\mathbf{f}(P)\coloneqq P\circ\mathbf{f}
is a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-linear map
\mathbf{f}(P)\colon H_{k,w}(K(p^{r});\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Hence by the Remark
2.5

\mathbf{f}(P)
represents a Hilbert cuspform
\mathbf{f}_{P}\in S_{k,w}(K(p^{r}),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
such that
\mathsf{a}_{p}(y,\mathbf{f}_{P})=\mathbf{f}(P)(\mathsf{T}(y^{\infty}))
for all
y\in\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{\times}F^{\times}_{\infty,+}
. In this article we use both
\mathbf{f}_{P}
and
\mathbf{f}(P)
to represent this cuspform. Moreover
\mathbf{f}_{p}
is nearly ordinay at
p
i.e.
\mathbf{f}_{P}
is normalized eigenform and for all
\mathfrak{p}\mid p
, the
T(\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}})
-eigenvalue is a
p
-adic unit. In fact, for any Hecke eigenform
f\in S_{k,w}(K(p^{r}),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, there exist some character
\Psi
and a
\Lambda_{F,\Psi}
-algebra
\mathcal{R}
such that the
p
-stabilization
f^{(p)}=\mathbf{f}_{P}
for some Hida family
\mathbf{f}\in\overline{\mathbf{S}}_{F}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}(K;\mathcal{R})
and
P\in\mathcal{W}_{\Psi}(\mathcal{R})
. That
\mathbf{f}
is called a branch of Hida family along
f^{(p)}
.

Definition 2.9
.

The set of Crystalline point
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\mathcal{R})
is the subset of
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{R})
consisting of the arithmetic points
P
such that
P_{\mid\Lambda_{F,\Psi}}\equiv P_{k,w,\psi,1\mid\Lambda_{F,\Psi}}
and the eigenform
\mathbf{f}_{P}
is
p
-old.

2.3.
Galois Representation Associated to Families of Hilbert Cuspforms

Let
\Psi
be a character of
\mathrm{cl}_{F}^{+}(\mathfrak{N}p)
which induces a character of
\mathbb{G}_{F}(\mathfrak{N})_{\mathrm{tor}}
and a Galois character. Both are denoted by
\Psi
. Suppose
\mathbf{f}\in\overline{\mathbf{S}}_{F}^{\mathrm{n.o.}}(\mathfrak{N},\Psi;\mathcal{R})
is an ordinary Hida family passing through
p
-stabilization
f^{(p)}
of a Hilbert cuspform
f
. The universal weight character

\mathbbl{x}\colon G_{F,\Sigma}\rightarrow W\rightarrow O\llbracket W\rrbracket^{\times}\rightarrow\Lambda_{F,\Psi}^{\times}

is induced via global class field theory, gives rise to the character

\mathbbl{x}_{\mathbf{f}}\colon G_{F,\Sigma}\xrightarrow{\mathbbl{x}}\Lambda_{F,\Psi}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}^{\times}.

For any
P\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{R})
, let
\mathrm{wt}(P)=P_{\mid\Lambda_{F,\Psi}}
. Hence we have
P\circ\mathbbl{x}_{\mathbf{f}}=\mathrm{wt}(P)\circ\mathbbl{x}
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}=\operatorname{Frac}(\mathcal{R})
, then by
[
Hid89a
, Theorem 1]
, there is a unique big Galois representation
\rho_{\mathbf{f}}
associated with
\mathbf{f}
acting on
V_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}\cong\mathcal{Q}^{2}
. In particular,
\rho_{\mathbf{f}}
is continuous with
\operatorname{im}(\rho_{\mathbf{f}})\subset\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{R}}(T_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger})
and satisfying

(2.7)

\bigwedge^{2}\rho_{\mathbf{f}}\equiv\mathcal{R}(\mathbbl{x}_{\mathbf{f}}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl},F}^{-1}\Psi_{\mathbf{f}})

where
T_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}
is an
\mathcal{R}
-lattice inside
V_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}
. Moreover
\rho_{\mathbf{f}}
also satisfies

(2.8)

\det(1-\rho_{\mathbf{f}}(\operatorname{Fr}(\mathfrak{q}))X)=1-\mathbf{f}(\mathsf{T}(\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}}))X+\Psi(\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}})[\xi_{\varpi_{\mathfrak{q}}}^{-t_{F}}]\operatorname{N}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathfrak{q}X^{2}

for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{q}\nmid\mathfrak{N}p
.
We will assume that

(Irr)

The residual representation of
\rho_{\mathbf{f}}
is absolutely irreducible.

By
[
Wil88
, Theorem 2.2.2]
, we have for any prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\mid p
, there is an exact sequence of
G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-modules

0\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}\rightarrow T_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}\rightarrow 0

such that each
F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\pm}V_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}
is rank one
\mathcal{R}
-module. Hence we get

\rho_{\mathbf{f}}|_{G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}\delta_{\mathbf{f},\mathfrak{p}}&*\\
0&\alpha_{\mathbf{f},\mathfrak{p}}\end{pmatrix}

where
\alpha_{\mathbf{f},\mathfrak{p}}\colon G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}^{\times}
is the unramified character such that
\alpha_{\mathbf{f},\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{Fr}_{\mathfrak{p}})
is a
p
-adic unit and root of (
2.8
). Also assume the following hypothesis

(Dist)

\delta_{\mathbf{f},\mathfrak{p}}\not\equiv\alpha_{\mathbf{f},\mathfrak{p}}\mod\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{R}}
for all
\mathfrak{p}\mid p
.

Remark 2.10
.

These two hypothesis
(Irr)
and
(Dist)
make sure that
T_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}
is free of rank two as
\mathcal{R}
-module and
\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\pm}T_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}
rank 1 free module for all
\mathfrak{p}\mid p
.

Assume that, there exist a character
\Psi^{-\frac{1}{2}}
such that
(\Psi^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{2}=\Psi
. Let
V_{\mathbf{f}}=V_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathbbl{x}_{\mathbf{f}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Psi^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}
, then there is an isomorphism of
\operatorname{Frac}(\mathcal{R})\llbracket G_{F,\Sigma}\rrbracket
-modules

V_{\mathbf{f}}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{\mathbf{f}}^{*}(1)=\operatorname{Hom}(V,\operatorname{Frac}(\mathcal{R}))(1).

Therefore,
V_{\mathbf{f}}
is a self-dual representation containing self-dual stable lattice

(2.9)

T_{\mathbf{f}}\coloneqq T_{\mathbf{f}}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathbbl{x}_{\mathbf{f}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Psi^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{\mathrm{cycl}}.

Hence the lattice
T_{\mathbf{f}}
fits in an exact sequence of
\mathcal{R}[G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}]
-modules

0\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\rightarrow T_{\mathbf{f}}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\rightarrow 0.

Any Hida family in our article will satisfy
(Irr)
and
(Dist)
and admits a self dual free lattice defined in (
2.9
).

2.4.
Tensor Induction Representations

Let
H
be an index two subgroup of finite group
G
. Suppose
k
is a field and
\rho
is a representation of
H
acting on a finite
k
-vector space
V
.

Definition 2.11
.

The tensor induction of
\rho
is defined as

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Ind}^{\otimes}_{G/H}

\displaystyle\rho)\colon G\rightarrow\operatorname{Aut}_{k}(V\otimes V)

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Ind}^{\otimes}_{G/H}

\displaystyle\rho)(h)(v_{1}\otimes v_{2})=\rho(h)(v_{1})\otimes\rho(\theta h\theta)(v_{2}),

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Ind}^{\otimes}_{G/H}

\displaystyle\rho)(\theta)(v_{1}\otimes v_{2})=v_{2}\otimes v_{1},

for all
h\in H
, where
\{1,\theta\}
is right coset of
H
.

Let
(\rho^{\theta},V^{\theta})
is the representation obtained via conjugation by
\theta
(i.e.
\rho^{\theta}(h)=\rho(\theta h\theta^{-1})
for all
h\in H
) and as a vector space
V^{\theta}=V
. Hence
(\operatorname{Ind}^{\otimes}_{G/H}\rho)_{\mid H}\equiv\rho\otimes\rho^{\theta}
.

Proposition 2.12
.

Let
(\rho,V)
be a self-dual representation, then
\left((\operatorname{Ind}^{\otimes}_{G/H}\rho),V\otimes V^{\theta}\right)
is reducible if and only if
V^{*}(\lambda)\cong V^{\theta}
for some character
\lambda
. Moreover
V\otimes V^{\theta}\cong\operatorname{ad}^{0}(V)(\lambda)\oplus k(\lambda)
for some character
\lambda
.

The decomposition type of
(\operatorname{Ind}^{\otimes}_{G/H}\rho)
is
(3,1)
when the former is reducible
[
For19
, Proposition 2.3]
.
Let
F/\mathbb{Q}
be a totally real quadratic number field and
\mathbf{g}
be a
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
-adic Hida family of Hilbert cuspforms of level
\mathfrak{N}_{\mathbf{g}}
and character
\Psi_{\mathbf{g}}
. Suppose

\Sigma\coloneqq\{\upsilon\mid\mathfrak{d}_{F}\mathfrak{N}_{\mathbf{g}}p\infty\}

be a finite set of places of
F
, then
G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma}\coloneqq\operatorname{Gal}(F^{\Sigma}/\mathbb{Q})
where
F^{\Sigma}
is the maximal extension of
F
unramified outside
\Sigma
and
G_{F,\Sigma}\coloneqq\operatorname{Gal}(F^{\Sigma}/F)
. Therefore we have
G_{F,\Sigma}\triangleleft G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma}
and
G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma}/G_{F,\Sigma}
is canonically isomorphic with
\operatorname{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})=\{1,\theta\}
. Let
\rho_{\mathbf{g}}\colon G_{F,\Sigma}\rightarrow\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}})
be the self dual big Galois representation associated with
\mathbf{g}
acting on rank two
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
-lattice
T_{\mathbf{g}}
. We can extend the notion of tensor induction on Galois representation via projective limit. So we have

Proposition 2.13
.

Suppose
\operatorname{As}(\rho_{\mathbf{g}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Ind}^{\otimes}_{G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma}/G_{F,\Sigma}}(\rho_{\mathbf{g}})(-1)
. If
\mathbf{g}
has a trivial central character (i.e.
\Psi_{\mathbf{g}}\equiv\mathds{1}
) and
\rho_{\mathbf{g}}^{\theta}\cong\rho_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}(\dot{\lambda})
for some character
\dot{\lambda}
(either quadratic or trivial) then
\operatorname{As}(\rho_{\mathbf{g}})|_{G_{F,\Sigma}}\cong\operatorname{ad}^{0}(\rho_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\oplus\lambda
by setting
\lambda=\dot{\lambda}(-1)
.

Remark 2.14
.

The representation
\operatorname{As}(\rho_{\mathbf{g}})
is isomorphic to Asai-representation of
\mathbf{g}
. When
\mathbf{g}
is a base change family of some Hida family of cuspform
\mathbf{g}^{\flat}
over
\mathbb{Q}
, then there may exist a character
\lambda\colon G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}\subset\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}^{\times}
associated with
F/\mathbb{Q}
via global class field theory such that
\operatorname{As}(\rho_{\mathbf{g}})
becomes Asai-decomposable. For more general cases the family
\mathbf{g}
need not be decomposable but for some finite set of arithmetic points in
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{g}}=\operatorname{Spf}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}})(\mathbb{C}_{p})
, the specialization of
\mathbf{g}
may be decomposable. (cf.
[
Liu16
, Section 3.2]
)

We have the representation space of
\operatorname{As}(\rho_{\mathbf{g}})
as rank four
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
-module
T_{\mathbf{g}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{\theta}
. By assumptions we have isomorphism of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}\left\llbracket G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma}\right\rrbracket
-modules
T_{\mathbf{g}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{\theta}\cong T_{\mathbf{g}}\widehat{\otimes}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}(\lambda)\cong\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\coloneqq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}(T_{\mathbf{g}},T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)
since
T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}\coloneqq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}(T_{\mathbf{g}},\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}})\cong T_{\mathbf{g}}
. For
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}
,

\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)
acquires filtration of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}\llbracket G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}\rrbracket
-submodule

(2.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\supset\underbrace{\ker\{\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}})\}(\lambda)}_{=:\mathrm{Fil}^{3}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)}\supset\underbrace{\operatorname{Hom}(T_{\mathbf{g}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)}_{=:\mathrm{Fil}^{2}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)}\qquad\qquad\qquad\\
\supset\underbrace{\operatorname{Hom}(\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)}_{=:\mathrm{Fil}^{1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)}\supset\{0\}\end{aligned}

2.5.
Decomposition of Big Galois Modules

Let
\mathbf{f}
(resp.
\mathbf{g}
) be an ordinary Hida family of cuspforms on
\cal G
of level
\mathfrak{N}_{\mathbf{f}}
(resp.
\mathfrak{N}_{\mathbf{g}}
) with branch
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}
(resp.
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
) and trivial central character. We also assume the finite prime
\mathfrak{p}\nmid\mathfrak{N}_{\mathbf{f}}\mathfrak{N}_{\mathbf{g}}
for
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}
and
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{g}
satisfies

Suppose

\Sigma
be a finite set of places of
F
containing all the places dividing
\mathfrak{d}_{F}\mathfrak{N}_{\mathbf{f}}\mathfrak{N}_{\mathbf{g}}p\infty
. Let
F^{\Sigma}
be the maximal extension of
F
unramified outside
\Sigma
, then we denote

\displaystyle G_{F,\Sigma}\coloneqq\operatorname{Gal}(F^{\Sigma}/F).

Thus we have self-dual, rank two
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}\left\llbracket G_{F,\Sigma}\right\rrbracket
-lattice (resp.
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}\left\llbracket G_{F,\Sigma}\right\rrbracket
-lattice)
T_{\mathbf{f}}
(resp.
T_{\mathbf{g}}
). We denote
\mathbf{g}^{\theta}
as a Hida family of Hilbert cuspforms such that associated Galois representation
T_{\mathbf{g}^{\theta}}
is isomorphic to the
\theta
-twist
T_{\mathbf{g}}^{\theta}
(i.e
\rho_{\mathbf{g}^{\theta}}(h)=\rho_{\mathbf{g}}(\theta h\theta^{-1})
).
Let
G_{F,\Sigma}
act on
T_{\mathbf{g}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{\theta}
via
\operatorname{As}(\rho_{\mathbf{g}})_{|G_{F,\Sigma}}
. We set the regular, local ring

\mathcal{R}\coloneqq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}.

In this setup, let
\mathbf{T}_{3}=T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}T_{\mathbf{g}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{\theta}
be the
\mathcal{R}
-lattice of rank eight, then

\boldsymbol{\rho}_{3}\coloneqq\rho_{\mathbf{f}}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\rho_{\mathbf{g}})_{|G_{F,\Sigma}}\colon G_{F,\Sigma}\rightarrow\operatorname{GL}_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbf{T}_{3})\cong\operatorname{GL}_{8}(\mathcal{R})

is a self-dual Galois representation. The associated weight space is

\mathcal{W}\coloneqq\operatorname{Spf}(\mathcal{R})(\mathbb{C}_{p})=\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}})\times\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}).

The subset of arithmetic points is denoted by
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}})\times\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}})\subset\mathcal{W}
. Furthermore
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}
has following disjoint subsets:

•

The set of totally
\mathbf{f}
-dominated points

\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\mathbf{f}}\coloneqq\{(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}\colon m(P)>2m(Q)\}.

•

The set of
\mathbf{f}
-dominated at
\tau_{1}
points

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}\coloneqq\left\{(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}\colon\begin{aligned} k(P)_{\tau_{1}}\geq 2k(Q)_{\tau_{1}},\ k(P)_{\tau_{2}}<2k(Q)_{\tau_{2}},\qquad\qquad\\
m(P)=2m(Q),\text{ and }k(P)_{\tau_{2}}+2k(Q)_{\tau_{2}}\text{ is even}\end{aligned}\right\}.

•

The set of
\mathbf{f}
-dominated at
\tau_{2}
points

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{(\mathrm{bal},\mathbf{f})}\coloneqq\left\{(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}\colon\begin{aligned} k(P)_{\tau_{2}}\geq 2k(Q)_{\tau_{2}},\ k(P)_{\tau_{1}}<2k(Q)_{\tau_{1}},\qquad\qquad\\
m(P)=2m(Q),\text{ and }k(P)_{\tau_{1}}+2k(Q)_{\tau_{1}}\text{ is even}\end{aligned}\right\}.

•

The set of totally balanced points

\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\mathrm{bal}}\coloneqq\{(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}\colon m(P)<2m(Q)\}.

2.5.1.

Let us consider the natural
O
-algebra homomorphisms

(2.11)

\begin{split}i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}:\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}&;\quad a\mapsto a\otimes 1;\\
i^{*}_{\mathbf{g}}:\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}&;\quad b\mapsto 1\otimes b;\end{split}

which also induces surjective morphisms

\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathbf{f}}}\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}),\quad\mathcal{W}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathbf{g}}}\mathcal{W}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}).

Suppose
T_{\mathbf{g}}^{\theta}\cong T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}(\dot{\lambda})
for some quadratic character
\dot{\lambda}\colon G_{F,\Sigma}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}\subset\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
. By abuse of notation, we also define the characters
\dot{\lambda}\colon G_{F,\Sigma}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}\subset\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}
and
\dot{\lambda}\colon G_{F,\Sigma}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}\subset\mathcal{R}
taking similar value as the first
\dot{\lambda}
. Therefore we have self-dual
\mathcal{R}\llbracket G_{F,\Sigma}\rrbracket
-module
\mathbf{T}_{1}\coloneqq T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}(\lambda)\cong T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)\otimes_{i_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}}\mathcal{R}
of rank two and
\mathbf{T}_{2}\coloneqq T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)
of rank six where
\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})
is the trace zero matrices of
\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})
and
\lambda=\dot{\lambda}(-1)
. Furthermore, we get that
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{3}
is decomposable whenever
\mathbf{f}\text{ and }\mathbf{g}
both have a trivial central character. Suppose
\operatorname{tr}
is the map explicitly given by

\operatorname{tr}\colon T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{trace}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R}_{g}(\lambda).

We then have an exact sequence

(2.12)

0\rightarrow\mathbf{T}_{2}\xrightarrow{\iota}\mathbf{T}_{3}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{tr}}\mathbf{T}_{1}\rightarrow 0.

where
\iota\coloneqq\operatorname{id_{\mathbf{f}}\otimes i}\colon T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\hookrightarrow T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)
is the canonical embedding. The self-duality of
\mathbf{T}_{1},\mathbf{T}_{2}
and
\mathbf{T}_{3}
gives us

(2.13)

0\rightarrow\mathbf{T}_{1}\xrightarrow{\tr}\mathbf{T}_{3}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\tr}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\rightarrow 0

where the map
\tr
is given by transposing the map
\operatorname{tr}
and using the self-duality of the modules. Both exact sequences (
2.12
) and (
2.13
) are split. We denote the representations as
\boldsymbol{\rho_{1}}
and
\boldsymbol{\rho_{2}}
associated to
\mathbf{T}_{1}
and
\mathbf{T}_{2}
respectively.

Remark 2.15
.

Suppose
\omega_{\lambda}
be a the adelic character of conductor
\mathfrak{N}_{\lambda}
associated with
\lambda
. Then we can get a Hida family
\mathbf{f}\otimes\omega_{\lambda}
such that
(\mathbf{f}\otimes\omega_{\lambda})_{P}=\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\omega_{\lambda}
for all
P\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}})
. We also have
T_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\omega_{\lambda}}\cong T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)
for associated Galois representations
[
Hid91
, §7F]
. We will denote this family as
\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda
for simplicity.

2.6.
The Artin Formalism

Let
(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}
be a pair of crystalline points. Hence we can get

f\in S_{k(P),w(P)}(\mathfrak{N}_{f},\psi_{f})\text{ and }g\in S_{k(Q),w(Q)}(\mathfrak{N}_{g},\psi_{g})

such that
f=\mathbf{f}_{P}
and
g=\mathbf{g}_{Q}
. Thus we can get an automorphic representation
g^{\theta}
as the base change of
g
by the field extension
F/\mathbb{Q}
. Let us denote
\operatorname{As}(g)
be the automorphic representation
g\otimes g^{\theta}
on
\cal G
.

2.6.1.
L
-functions and Root numbers

The complete Rankin-Selberg
L
-function satisfies the functional equation

(2.14)

L(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g),s)=\varepsilon(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))N(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))^{-s}L(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g),c(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))-s)

for the global root number
\varepsilon(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))\in\{\pm 1\}
, some constant
N(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and the central critical value
c(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))
.
Suppose
\omega_{\lambda}
is the associated quadratic adelic character of
\operatorname{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
to
\lambda
. Then according to our assumption
g
has a trivial central character and is Asai decomposable i.e
g^{\theta}\cong g\otimes\omega_{\lambda}
. Therefor we have
Artin formalism
(in other words the factorization of
L
-functions)

(2.15)

L(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g),\cdot)=L(f\otimes\breve{g},\cdot)L(f\otimes\omega_{\lambda},\cdot)

where
\breve{g}=\operatorname{ad}^{0}g\otimes\omega_{\lambda}
. We also have a factorization of global root numbers

(2.16)

\varepsilon(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))=\varepsilon(f\otimes\breve{g})\varepsilon(f\otimes\omega_{\lambda}).

Note that following Remark
2.15
, we have
f\otimes\omega_{\lambda}=\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\lambda
.

Remark 2.16
.

The global root numbers are defined as the product of local root numbers. The equation (
2.16
) also holds in the local cases. In other words we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))=\varepsilon(f\otimes\breve{g})\varepsilon(f\otimes\omega_{\lambda}),\quad\forall v\mid\mathfrak{N}_{f}\mathfrak{N}_{g}\infty.

2.6.2.
p
-adic Artin formalism

Our main goal in this present article is to establish some
p
-adic analogues of the factorization (
2.15
) in the algebraic setup. Here we briefly discuss the associated
p
-adic
L
-functions.
For example in
[
BM19
, Theorem 1.2]
, a triple product
p
-adic
L
-functions

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\spadesuit}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))\colon\mathcal{W}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p},

satisfying interpolation properties

(2.17)

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\spadesuit}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))(P,Q)\doteq\dfrac{L(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))}{\Omega^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g})}},\quad(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}\cap\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\spadesuit},

where
\Omega^{\spadesuit}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g})}
is the associated period,
\spadesuit\in\{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal}),(\mathrm{bal},\mathbf{f})\}
(the interpolation factors are precisely defined in
[
BM19
]
and
[
Mol21
]
). In this philosophy, we can conjecturally say that (in a broad sense) there exist
p
-adic
L
-functions

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))\colon\mathcal{W}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p},

satisfying interpolation properties

(2.18)

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))(P,Q)\doteq\dfrac{L(f\otimes\operatorname{As}(g))}{\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g})}},\quad(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}\cap\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\bullet},

for the suitable period
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g})}
,
\bullet=\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal}
. In this manner we expect conjectural
p
-adic
L
-functions of this forms

(2.19)

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})(P,Q)\doteq\dfrac{L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}}_{Q})}{\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}}}},\quad(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}\cap\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\bullet},

for some suitable period
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}}}
and
\bullet\in\{\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal},(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal}),(\mathrm{bal},\mathbf{f})\}
. We also have the
p
-adic
L
-functions for Hida family of cuspforms
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda)
(see
[
Dim13
, Theorem 0.1]
or
[
BH24
, §8.2]
). Hence we have a conjecture

Conjecture 1
.

In full generalities, we should have a factorization like

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})\cdot\mathscr{L}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda).

2.6.3.
Root numbers and vanishing of
L
-functions

In this article, we are interested in the case when
\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\lambda)=0
. The interpolative properties suggest that

\mathscr{L}_{p}(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\lambda)=0.

In the present paper, we mainly deal with two situations as given by the following data satisfying (
2.16
)

\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}_{Q}))

\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}}_{Q})

(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}^{\mathbf{f}}

-1

+1

(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}

+1

-1

We remark that for
(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}^{(\mathrm{bal},\mathbf{f})}
the problem can be addressed in a manner similar to in the similar manner of
(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}
.

2.6.4.

We can further say that for
(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\mathrm{bal}}
one can predict
\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}_{Q}))=-1
(cf.
[
DR22
,
BSV22
]
). Hence via (
2.15
) we can infer that either
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathrm{bal}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})=0
(if
\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}\otimes\check{\varphi})=+1
)
or
\mathscr{L}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda)=0
(if
\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}\otimes\check{\varphi})=-1
).
In all cases, we have the rather uninteresting factorization of the totally balanced
p
-adic
L
-functions

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathrm{bal}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))=0=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathrm{bal}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})\cdot\mathscr{L}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda).

2.7.
Factorization of
\mathscr{L}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))
in algebraic setup

The factorization of the
\mathbf{f}
-dominant at
\tau_{1}

p
-adic
L
-functions

(2.23)

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})\cdot\mathscr{L}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda)

reduces, once we are provided with the expected interpolation properties of various
p
-adic
L
-functions, to a comparison of periods at the specializations of both sides at
(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}
.We are addressing the problem when
\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda)=-1
. In this scenario, at the central critical value
c=c(P,Q)
, (
2.15
) and (
2.6.3
) give

\operatorname{ord}_{s=c}L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g})_{Q})\geq 2,\quad\text{ if }(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}

In particular, we get by taking a derivative at the central critical value

(2.24)

\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}_{Q}),s)=\frac{d}{ds}L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}}_{Q},s)\frac{d}{ds}L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\lambda,s)

Inspired by the work of
[
BS25
]
and the fundamental result in
[
BDP13
,
DR22
,
BSV22
]
, the guiding principle of our project is

(BDP1)

the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathscr{L}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))
should be thought of as a
p
-adic avatar of the family of second-order derivatives

\left\{\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}_{Q}),s)\mid_{s=c}\colon(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}\right\};

(BDP2)

the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathscr{L}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})
should be thought of as a
p
-adic avatar of the family of first order derivatives

\left\{\frac{d}{ds}L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}}_{Q},s)\mid_{s=c}\colon(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}\right\}.

Remark 2.17
.

The general philosophy of
Bertolini-Darmon-Prasanna
in
[
BDP13
]
the
BDP

p
-adic
L
-function as a
p
-adic avatar of the derivatives
of complex analytic
L
-functions at the central critical points using the (generalized) Heegner cycles. Using this circle of ideas, the authors in
[
BSV22
,
DR22
]
suggest that the unbalanced
p
-adic
L
-functions should be considered as
p
-adic avatars of the derivatives of the Garrett–Rankin L-series at the central critical point. Our forthcoming work will be about further pursuing this philosophy in a totally real number field setup.

Based on the principles
(BDP1)
and
(BDP2)
, we propose the following refinement of the factorization (
2.23
):

Conjecture 2
.

Suppose that
\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda)=-1
and the root number condition holds, we have the following factorization of
p
-adic
L
-functions (up to some
p
-adic meromorphic function)

(2.25)

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))(P,Q)=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})(P,Q)\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{\rm LZ})\right).

Here
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm LZ}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}
is the Kolyvagin system coming from the Euler system constructed by Loeffler and Zerbes in
[
LZ20a
]
. At this moment the conjecture is out of reach due to the scarcity of information related to the explicit
BDP
formulas regarding quadratic Hilbert modular forms and
p
-adic
L
-functions in use. However we show the algebraic counterpart of the Conjecture
2
that is compatible with the theme of Iwasawa main conjecture, in the sense that we have defined the (higher rank)
modules of leading term

\bbdelta_{r}(X,\Delta)
(concerning the relevant
Selmer complexes
and their
core rank
) in
Section

4
which can be thought of as an algebraic analogues of
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Theorem A

(
Theorem

5.14
below)
.

Let the root number conditions in Conjecture
2
hold. Suppose that the module of (algebraic)
p
-adic
L
-functions
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})
is non vanishing. Under certain hypotheses we have

(2.26)

\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})=\operatorname{Exp}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\left(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\right)\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm LZ}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda})\right).

Proving this conjecture is the main content of
Section

5
.

2.8.
Factorization of
\mathscr{L}^{\mathbf{g}}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))
in algebraic setup

Now we focus on the case
(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\mathbf{f}}
. Here we have a hypothesis that
L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\lambda)
has zero of order two and
L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\check{\mathbf{g}_{Q}})
does not vanish at the central critical value. Therefore we get from (
2.15
)

\operatorname{ord}_{s=c}L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g})_{Q})=2,\quad\text{ if }(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\mathbf{f}}

Similarly, we take our guiding principle as

(BDP3)

the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathscr{L}^{\mathbf{g}}_{p}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))
should be thought of as a
p
-adic avatar of the family of second order derivatives

\left\{\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}L(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}_{Q}),s)\mid_{s=c}\colon(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\mathbf{f}}\right\};

We assume that
\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\lambda
satisfies the Plectic conjecture. In particular, if we have a rank-two Kolyvagin system (cf.
[
BSS18
]
)
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2}
coming from rank two Euler system generated by generalized Kato classes. We remark that these types of rank two generalized Kato classes are more natural to appear in real quadratic number field setup (cf. Remark
3.13
). For more details kindly check Remark
6.1
. Hence similar to
Conjecture

2
we can get the following conjecture assuming that
\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda)=-1
and the root number condition holds true

Conjecture 3
.

We have the following factorization of
p
-adic
L
-functions

(2.27)

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))(P,Q)=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathrm{bal}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})(P,Q)\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}^{2}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2})\right).

Remark 2.18
.

Though the existence of
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2}
is conjectural, but there are some recent works towards the evidence of the higher rank Euler system. In
[
FG23
]
, the authors introduced
plectic
Stark-Heegner points for an elliptic curve
A/F
given that
\lim_{s\to 1}L(A/F,s)=2
which is related to the local image of
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2}
in a parallel weight 2 specializtion. Also the
“mock plectic points”
in
[
DF25
]
, may be seen an oblique evidence for the plectic philosophy of Nekovář and Scholl, using the mock analogue of Hilbert modular forms.

In the view of last remark we can observe that
Conjecture

3
is almost unaccessible by the informations we get from recent literature. Nevertheless we have shown the algebraic analogue of
Conjecture

3
in
Section

6
.

Theorem B

(
Theorem

6.12
and Corollary
6.13
below)
.

Let the root number conditions in Conjecture
3
hold. Suppose that the module of (algebraic)
p
-adic
L
-functions
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is non vanishing. Under certain hypotheses we have

(2.28)

\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}})\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}^{2}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2})\right).

Prooving
Theorem

A
and
Theorem

B
is the main part of this paper. In the next section, we discuss about Nekovar’s theory of Selmer complexes to define the (algebraic)
p
-adic
L
-functions more precisely.

3.
Selmer Complexes

We now introduce the algebraic counterparts of the (
p
-adic) analytic objects briefly discussed in the §
2.6
, §
2.7
, and §
2.8
. We will formulate the first step of algebraic factorization in this section. The ETNC
1
1

1

abbrv. Equivariant Tamagawa Number Conjecture.
philosophy will be our general guidance (cf
[
BS25
, Appendix A]
).

3.1.
Greenberg Conditions

For each
X\in\{\mathbf{T}_{1},\mathbf{T}_{2},\mathbf{T}_{3}\}
as in §
2.5
, with the fact
\mathbf{f}
,
\mathbf{g}
are
p
-ordinary families, with the conditions
(Irr)
and
(Dist)
gives rise to short exact sequences of
G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-modules (for
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}
) of the form

(3.1)

0\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}X\rightarrow X\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}X\rightarrow 0

We denote
\mathbbl{P}\coloneqq\{\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}\}\subset\Sigma
,
I_{v}\subset G_{F_{v}}
as the inertia subgroup and
\operatorname{Fr}_{v}\in G_{F_{v}}/I_{v}
as the (geometric) Frobenius element. Let’s define the following local conditions for a given short exact sequences like (
3.1
)(in the sense of
[
Nek06
]
)

U_{v}^{+}(X)=\begin{cases}C^{\bullet}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}X),&\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbbl{P};\\
C^{\bullet}(G_{F_{v}}/I_{v},X^{I_{F_{v}}}),&v\in\Sigma\backslash\mathbbl{P};\end{cases}

along with a morphism of complexes

i_{v}^{+}\colon U_{v}^{+}(X)\rightarrow C^{\bullet}(G_{F_{v}},X)\text{, for all }v\in\Sigma.

For each
v\in\Sigma\backslash\mathbbl{P}
, we have a quasi-isomorphism of complexes

U_{v}^{+}(X)\cong\left[X^{I_{v}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Fr}_{v}-1}X^{I_{v}}\right],

concentrated in degrees 0 and 1.

We note that even for a given
X
, there exists a number of choices of one-step filtration such as (
3.1
), which should be thought of as a reflection of the multiple-choice of
p
-adic
L
-functions, depending on the choice of interpolation.

Let
v\in\Sigma\backslash\mathbbl{P}
and suppose that

0\rightarrow M_{1}\rightarrow M_{2}\rightarrow M_{3}\rightarrow 0,

is a split short exact sequence continuous
G_{F_{v}}
-representations of free finite rank over a complete local Noetherian ring
R
. Then we have

(3.2)

U_{v}^{+}(M_{2})=U_{v}^{+}(M_{3})\oplus U_{v}^{+}(M_{1}).

3.1.1.
Important local conditions

The definition goes as follow

Definition 3.1
.

The data

\Delta\left(\left\{\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}X\right\}_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbbl{P}}\right)\coloneqq\{i_{v}^{+}\colon U_{v}^{+}(X)\rightarrow C^{\bullet}(G_{F_{v}},X)\text{, for all }v\in\Sigma\}

is called a Greenberg-local condition on
X
.

In the following examples, we shall record some useful Greenberg-local conditions for our purpose.

Example 3.2
.

Suppose
X=\mathbf{T}_{3}
. For
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\;\mathfrak{p}_{2}
, lets define

(1)

\mathbf{f}
-dominated Greenberg-local condition
\Delta_{\mathbf{f}}=\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{f}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}X,\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{f}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}X)
is

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{f}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3}\coloneqq\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{T}_{3}.

(2)

Balanced Greenberg-local condition
\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}}=\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}X,\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}X)
is

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3}\coloneqq\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{Fil}^{3}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)+T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{Fil}^{1}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{T}_{3}.

(3)

\mathbf{g}
-dominated Greenberg-local condition
\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}=\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}X,\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}X)
defined as

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3}\coloneqq T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{Fil}^{2}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda))\hookrightarrow\mathbf{T}_{3}.

(4)

We will also consider the Greenberg-local condition
\Delta_{+}\coloneqq\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}^{+}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}X,\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}^{+}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}X)
given by the following rank 5 direct submodule of
X

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}^{+}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3}=\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3}+\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3}.

Example 3.3
.

For
\mathbf{T}_{3}
we can define more intrinsic (mixed) local conditions

(1)

\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{f})}=\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{3},\;\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{f}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\mathbf{T}_{3})
.

(2)

\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}=\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{3},\;\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\mathbf{T}_{3})
.

(3)

\Delta_{(+,\mathrm{bal})}:=\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}^{+}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{3},\;\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\mathbf{T}_{3})
.

Remark 3.4
.

We remark that under the self-duality pairing
\mathbf{T}_{3}\otimes\mathbf{T}_{3}\rightarrow\mathcal{R}(1)
, the following local conditions discussed above are self-orthogonal complements.

\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}^{\perp}=\Delta_{\mathbf{g}},\quad\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}}^{\perp}=\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}},\quad\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}^{\perp}=\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}.

Now we shall propagate these local conditions to
\mathbf{T}_{2}
and
\mathbf{T}_{1}
via the exact sequence (
2.12
) and (
2.13
).

Example 3.5
.

When
X=\mathbf{T}_{2}
we define Greenberg local condition

\tr\Delta_{?}\coloneqq\Delta(\{\im(F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+?}\mathbf{T}_{3}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{T}_{3}\rightarrow\mathbf{T}_{2})\}_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbbl{P}})

(i)

\mathbf{f}
-dominated Greenberg-local condition
\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{f}}\coloneqq\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{f}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2})
is

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{f}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\coloneqq\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)

(ii)

Balanced Greenberg-local condition
\tr\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}}\coloneqq\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2})
is

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\coloneqq\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\underbrace{\ker\{\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}},F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}})\}}_{=:\mathrm{Fil}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})}(\lambda)\quad

\displaystyle+\quad T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\underbrace{\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}},F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}})}_{=:\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})}(\lambda)

(iii)

g
-dominated Greenberg-local condition
\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}\coloneqq\Delta(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2})
is

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\coloneqq T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\underbrace{\ker\{\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}},F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}})\}}_{=:\mathrm{Fil}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})}(\lambda)

Similarly, we can get the local conditions
\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}
,
\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{f})}
,
\tr\Delta_{(+,\mathrm{bal})}
etcetera.

Lemma 3.6
.

The following hold for any
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbbl{P}

(1)

\rank_{\mathcal{R}}\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{f}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}=\rank_{\mathcal{R}}\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}=3
.

(2)

\rank_{\mathcal{R}}\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}=4

(3)

We have the following short exact sequence of
G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-representations

(3.3)

0\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\otimes_{O}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}(\lambda)\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

Proof of (1) and (2) is immediate from the previous example.

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}
is a submodule of
\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}
and we get

\operatorname{coker}\left(\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\right)\cong\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\rm gr_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\rm Ad^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)

where
\mathrm{gr}_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\coloneqq\left(\mathrm{Fil}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})/\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\right)(\lambda)
. Hence we have to show

(3.4)

\mathrm{gr}_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\cong\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}.

Let
\{u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\}\subset F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}
denote an
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
-basis and let
\{u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}},u^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\}
be an extension. Let
\{\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}},\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\}\subset T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}
be the dual basis. Then

(3.5)

\mathrm{Fil}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})=\ker\{\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}},F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}})\}=\left\langle u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}},u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}-u^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right\rangle

and

\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})=\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}},F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}})=\left\langle u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right\rangle.

Let denote the
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
-valued character
\chi_{\pm}
of
G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
as the action of
\mathscr{F}^{\pm}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}}
. Then for any
g\in G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}

g\cdot u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}=\chi_{+}(g)u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}},\quad g\cdot\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}=\chi_{+}(g)^{-1}\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}

Hence
g\cdot(u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}})=u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}

and
g\cdot u^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}=\chi_{-}(g)u^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}+c(g)u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}
for some
c(g)\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
which gives
g\cdot\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}=\chi_{-}(g)\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Combining all of these, we get
g\cdot(u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}-u^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}})=u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}-u^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}
in
\mathrm{gr}_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})
, which proves (
3.4
).
∎

Definition 3.7
.

As we know
\mathbf{T}_{1}\cong T_{\mathbf{f}}\otimes_{O}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}(\lambda)
, let’s define Greenberg-local conditions on
\mathbf{T}_{1}
as

\tr\Delta_{?}\coloneqq\Delta(\{\ker(F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+?}\mathbf{T}_{3}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{T}_{3}\rightarrow\mathbf{T}_{2})\}_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbbl{P}}).

So we get

(i)

The filtration for Panchiskin local condition
\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{Pan}}\mathbf{T}_{1}\coloneqq\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\otimes_{O}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}(\lambda))
and we have the following correspondence for
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\;\mathfrak{p}_{2}

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{f}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1}=\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\lambda}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}=\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1}.

(ii)

Filtration for strict local condition

\mathscr{F}^{+0}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1}=\{0\}=\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1}.

Similarly, we can define the local condition
\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{f}}=\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}
,
\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}=\Delta_{0}
,
\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}=\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})}
etcetera of
\mathbf{T}_{1}
.

Definition 3.8
.

We define the (relaxed) Greenberg-local condition on
X
with the filtration as
\mathscr{F}^{+\emptyset}_{\mathfrak{p}}X=X
.

3.2.
Selmer Complexes associated with Greenberg-local conditions

Let
\Delta
be a Greenberg-local conditions on
X
, let us put

U_{\Sigma}^{+}(X)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{v\in\Sigma}U_{v}^{+}(X),\quad C^{\bullet}_{\Sigma}(X)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{v\in\Sigma}C^{\bullet}(G_{F_{v}},X)

and consider the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{\Sigma}^{+}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}(G_{F,\Sigma},X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\bullet}_{\Sigma}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\Sigma}^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\res_{\Sigma}}

where
i_{\Sigma}^{+}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{v\in\Sigma}i_{v}^{+}
and
\res_{\Sigma}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{v\in\Sigma}\res_{v}
.

Let’s define the Selmer complex associated with
(X,\Sigma,\Delta)

\widetilde{C}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\bullet}(G_{F,\Sigma},X,\Delta)\coloneqq\operatorname{Cone}(C^{\bullet}(G_{F,\Sigma},X)\oplus U_{\Sigma}^{+}(X)\xrightarrow{\res_{\Sigma}-i_{\Sigma}^{+}}C^{\bullet}_{\Sigma}(X))[-1]

define the corresponding object in derived category by
\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},X,\Delta)
and it’s cohomology by
\widetilde{H}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\bullet}(G_{F,\Sigma},X,\Delta)
.

Theorem 3.9
.

[
Nek06
, Theorem 7.8.6]

We have

\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},X,\Delta))=\sum_{v\mid\infty}\rank(X^{G_{v}})-\sum_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\rank(\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}X)

for the Euler-Poincare characteristic of
\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},X,\Delta)
.

3.2.1.

We get the following exact triangle, which can be thought of as the first step of our factorization statements

(3.6)

\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}\otimes\tr}\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{\pi_{\tr}}}\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\xrightarrow[+1]{\delta}

and

(3.7)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}\otimes\tr}\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{\pi_{\tr}}}\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\xrightarrow[+1]{\delta}

which will be studied extensively later. We also define the following:

Definition 3.10
.

—

(1)

Let
\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}
denote the composite map

(3.8)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\xrightarrow{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{2})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\operatorname{gr}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda))\\
\rightarrow H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\operatorname{gr}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda))\xrightarrow[(\ref{localses}.)]{\sim}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{2}/\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{2}),\end{aligned}

(2)

Let
\res_{/\mathrm{bal}}
denote the composite map

(3.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\xrightarrow{\res_{p}}\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g}}}\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\operatorname{gr}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda))\\
\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\operatorname{gr}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda))\xrightarrow[(\ref{localses}.)]{\sim}\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}/\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}),\end{aligned}

where the maps
\res_{p}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\res_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g}}\equiv\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g},\mathfrak{p}}
is the map induced from canonical surjection for all
\mathfrak{p}\mid p

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g},\mathfrak{p}}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\operatorname{gr}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda).

3.2.2.
Panchiskin defect

We now introduce the invariant called Panchiskin defect which is necessary to show the perfectness of certain Selmer complex. The foundational concept was introduced by Perrin-Riou and Rubin. Later Kazim Büyükboduk incorporated these ideas into the following articles
[
Büy09c
,
Büy09a
,
Büy09b
,
Büy10
,
Büy11a
,
Büy11b
]
. With the help of
[
LZ20b
, Section 7]
we will identify the differences between our present factorization problems with those in
[
Gre82
,
Pal18
,
BS25
]
.

Definition 3.11
.

Suppose that
X
is a free
O
-module of ﬁnite rank, endowed with a continuous
G_{F,\Sigma}
-action. We also have that
X
is equipped with a Greenberg local conditions
\Delta\coloneqq\Delta(\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}^{+}X,\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}^{+}X)
given by
G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}}
-stable submodule
\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}^{+}X\subset X
for
i=1,2
. We define the Panchiskin defect
\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(X,\Delta)
attached to the pair
(X,\Delta)
on setting

\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(X,\Delta)=\left\lvert\sum_{v\mid\infty}\rank(X^{c_{v}=1})-\sum_{i=1,2}\rank(\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}X)\right\rvert

where
X^{c_{v}=1}\subset X
is the
+1
-eigenspace of complex conjugation at
v
. We say that the pair
(X,\Delta)
is
r
-Panchiskin if
\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(X,\Delta)=r
.

Note that
X
is Panchishkin ordinary if there exist a Greenberg local condition
\Delta
such that
(X,\Delta)
is
0
-Panchiskin and all Hodge–Tate weights of
\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}^{+}X
(resp. of
X/\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}^{+}X
) are positive (resp. non-positive) for
i=1,2
.

Example 3.12
.

—

(1)

Let
x\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{(\mathbf{f},\mathrm{bal})}(O)
be an
O
-valued arithmetic point and let us denote
X(x):=X\otimes_{x}O
for
X=\mathbf{T}_{1},\mathbf{T}_{2},
and
\mathbf{T}_{3}
. Then

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(\mathbf{T}_{3}(x),\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})

\displaystyle=0;

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(\mathbf{T}_{2}(x),\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})

\displaystyle=1=\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(\mathbf{T}_{1}(x),\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}).

(2)

Let
x\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\mathbf{f}}(O)
be an
O
-valued arithmetic point and let us denote
X(x):=X\otimes_{x}O
for
X=\mathbf{T}_{1},\mathbf{T}_{2},
and
\mathbf{T}_{3}
. Then

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(\mathbf{T}_{3}(x),\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})

\displaystyle=0=\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(\mathbf{T}_{2}(x),\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}});

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(\mathbf{T}_{2}(x),\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})

\displaystyle=2=\delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}(\mathbf{T}_{1}(x),\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}).

Remark 3.13
.

Using the Panchiskin defect we can indicate why the factorization problem for algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions we consider in this article is considerably more challenging than those problems considered earlier in
[
Gre82
,
Pal18
,
BS25
]
. All the factorizations result in op. cite are obtained when the associated pairs are either
0
-Panchiskin or
1
-Panchiskin while we also consider the
2
-Panchiskin pairs (cf.
[
BS25
, §4.3.2]
). This discussion also gives evidence towards the existence of higher rank Euler systems assumed in
Section

6
(cf.
[
LZ20b
]
).

3.3.
Remarks on Tamagawa Factors

Let
v\nmid p\infty
be a place in
F
. Suppose
\varphi\colon G_{F_{v}}\rightarrow\operatorname{GL}_{R}(V)
be a continuous
G_{F_{v}}
-representation, where
V
is a free module of finite rank over a complete Noetherian local ring
R
with finite residue field of characteristic
p
.

Remark 3.14
.

When
R=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
F=\mathbb{Q}
, the
p
-adic valuation of the order of
H^{1}(I_{v},V)^{\mathrm{Fr}_{v}=1}_{R-\mathrm{tors}}
is the local Tamagawa factor at
v
(see
[
FP94
, §I.4.2.2]
). So it vanishes if
H^{1}(I_{v},V)
is a free
R
-module. In this philosophy, we would concentrate on the following conditions:

•

The
R
-module
H^{1}(I_{v},V)
is free.

This discussion about the Tamagawa factor is needed for verification that our Selmer complexes are perfect. Our arguments are inspired by the observations made by Büyükboduk et al. in
[
BS25
]
.

3.3.1.

Let
I_{v}^{(p)}\triangleleft I_{v}
be the unique subgroup satisfying
I_{v}/I_{v}^{(p)}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Let us fix a topological generator
t\in I_{v}/I_{v}^{(p)}
. (cf.
[
NSW08
, §7.5.2]
)

Lemma 3.15
.

—

(1)

The
R
-module
V^{I_{v}^{(p)}}
is free of finite rank.

(2)

The complex
C^{\bullet}(I_{v},V)
is quasi-isomorphic to the perfect complex

\cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow V^{I_{v}^{(p)}}\xrightarrow{t-1}V^{I_{v}^{(p)}}\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots

concentrated in degrees
0
and
1
.

(3)

For any ring homomorphism
R\rightarrow S
, the induced map
V^{I_{v}^{(p)}}\otimes_{R}S\rightarrow(V\otimes_{R}S)^{I_{v}^{(p)}}
