{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[(\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(i_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}^{+}(x_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}})))_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\right]}
\in
\in
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{1}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}}
\in
\in

where the morphism
\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}
is induced from the exact sequence

\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})})\rightarrow\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})\xrightarrow{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}R\Gamma(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})\xrightarrow{\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}}[+1]

In particular

H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})\ni[a_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}]\xmapsto{\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}}[(0,0,(\{0\}_{v\in\Sigma\backslash\{\mathfrak{p}_{1}\}},\{a_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\}))]\in\widetilde{H}_{\mathrm{f}}^{2}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})}).

Corollary 5.12
.

Suppose
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\neq 0
. then the map
\delta^{1}
factors as

(5.20)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\res_{/\mathrm{pan},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}}

and all the
\mathcal{R}
-modules that appear are of rank one.

Proof.

\delta^{1}
is Injective because
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})=0
since
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\neq 0
via Theorem
4.11
.

Previous Proposition tells that
\delta^{1}
factors as

(5.21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g}}\circ\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}}

Also
\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}
factors as

(5.22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}}

Hence we have a injective homomorphism
\res_{/\mathrm{Pan},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}
which is the composition of

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g}}\circ\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})\twoheadrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}.

Since
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})=\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}})
we have the required result.
∎

Recall our map
\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}
(see
3.8
) given as composite of

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g}}\circ\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})\rightarrow H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R}.

Hence
\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}
factors as

\displaystyle\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\colon\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\xrightarrow{\res_{/\mathrm{Pan},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\rightarrow H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}(\lambda)).

Since
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})=\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}})
in our present set-up, we have the following exact sequence for
\mathfrak{p}_{1}

0\rightarrow\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}\rightarrow\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}\rightarrow H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})\rightarrow 0.

As the map
\res_{/\mathrm{Pan}}
is injective and
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is torsion-free,

\res_{/\mathrm{Pan},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\subset\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}

is also torsion-free.
Since

\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}

is torsion

\res_{/\mathrm{Pan},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))
maps isomorphically onto its image

\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))\subset H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1}).

Hence we have an exact sequence

(5.23)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} 0\rightarrow\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}\rightarrow\dfrac{\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}}{\res_{/\mathrm{Pan},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\qquad\qquad\qquad\\
\rightarrow\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\rightarrow 0.\end{aligned}

Proposition 5.13
.

In the setting of Corollary
5.12
, we have

\displaystyle\Char\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})})}{\delta^{1}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\right)

\displaystyle=\Char\left(\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})}\right)\Char\left(\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\right).

Proof.

We have an exact sequence

(5.24)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} 0\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})\xrightarrow{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})\xrightarrow{\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}}\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})})\qquad\qquad\\
\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})\rightarrow 0.\end{aligned}

Hence we get via Corollary
5.12

(5.25)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\dfrac{\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{{\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan}}}))}}{\res_{/\mathrm{Pan},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\xrightarrow{\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}}\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(0,\mathrm{Pan})})}{\delta^{1}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})\rightarrow 0.

Thanks to equation
5.23
and Theorem
4.12
, we have

(5.26)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\Char\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})}{\delta^{1}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\\
&=\Char\left(\dfrac{\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{{\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan}}}))}}{\res_{/\mathrm{Pan},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\right)\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))\\
&=\Char\left(\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})}))}\right)\Char\left(\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\Char\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})}{\mathcal{R}\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})}\right)\\
&=\Char\left(\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})}\right)\Char\left(\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\right)\end{aligned}

as required.
∎

Theorem 5.14
.

Suppose
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\neq 0
. Then:

\displaystyle\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})=\operatorname{Exp}^{*}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}\left(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\right)\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm LZ}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda})\right).

Proof.

From the equation
3.6
, we get an exact sequence

(5.27)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} 0\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\xrightarrow{\delta^{1}}\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0,\mathrm{Pan}})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\\
\noindent\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\rightarrow 0\end{aligned}

Using Theorem
4.18
and Proposition
5.13
shows that

(5.28)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\Char\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\right)\\
&=\Char\left(\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{(\emptyset,\mathrm{Pan})})}\right)\Char\left(\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}))}\right)\end{aligned}

Hence we have via (
5.10
)

(5.29)

\displaystyle\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})=\operatorname{Exp}^{*}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}\left(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\right)\cdot i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm LZ}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda})\right).

We also get
\operatorname{Exp}^{*}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}\left(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\right)\neq 0\neq i^{*}_{\mathbf{f}}\left(\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm LZ}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda})\right)
.
∎

Remark 5.15
.

As previously noted, we expect that (in view of Iwasawa–Greenberg main conjectures)
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})
is generated by
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))(P,Q)
and
\operatorname{Exp}^{*}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}\left(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})})\right)
is generated by
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal})}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})(P,Q)
. Whereas
\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm LZ}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda})
is expected to interpolate the derivatives of Perrin-Riou’s
\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(V_{f\otimes\lambda})\otimes\cal{H}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{cycl}})
-valued
p
-adic
L
-function of
f\otimes\lambda
, a crystalline specialization of
\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda
(cf.
[
Per93
,
Per00
]
). The image
\operatorname{Log}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm LZ}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda})
coincide with the
p
-adic
L
-function in
[
Dim13
, Theorem 0.1]
by the main work of
[
LZ20a
]
.

Moreover, we also remark that
‘super-factorization’
(in the sense of
[
BC25
]
) of our main results in
Section

5
is a project in progress with considering CM forms. This would give analogous results of Theorem 3.4 of
[
BC25
]
in our setup. But the main challenges arise about construction of triple product
p
-adic
L
-functions for quadratic Hilbert modular forms in full generalities and getting the
‘BDP’
-principles. The authors are currently pursuing in this direction.

Please note that this situation is a real quadratic version of
rank (1,1)
case stated in
[
DR16
, §4.5.2]
.

6.
Factorization of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions (The rank (0,2) case)

6.1.
Modules of leading terms in rank (0,2) case

As in
Section

5
, we will list down all the hypotheses at the beginning.

6.1.1.
Hypotheses

Throughout
Section

6
we will work under the following assumption. Both
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{g}
satisfy
(Dist)
. While we assume
(Irr)
for
\mathbf{f}
, the family
\mathbf{g}
will satisfy the stronger version
(Irr+)
. Along with those, the condition
(TAM)
holds for
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{g}
to ensure
(

H
0

)
and
(Tam)
for
\mathbf{T}_{1}
,
\mathbf{T}_{2}
and
\mathbf{T}_{3}
. In this section, we work in the scenario of
Section

2.8
i.e.

(S2)

\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\lambda)=-1
and
\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}_{P}\otimes\operatorname{ad}^{0}(\mathbf{g}_{Q})(\lambda))=1
whenever
(P,Q)\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\mathbf{f}}
for the global root number.

For
\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda
we assume the following plectic conjecture to get a rank two Euler system:

(Plec)

There exist two distinct nontrivial plectic Siegel classes
\mathfrak{g}_{f\otimes\lambda,1}^{\rm plec}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{f\otimes\lambda,2}^{\rm plec}
in the the first plectic-etale cohomology of Hilbert-Blumenthal abelian surface associated with
f\otimes\lambda
for any crystalline specialization
f
of
\mathbf{f}
. (cf.
[
NS16
, §15]
)

We also assume the hypotheses
(Ord)
,
(MC)
,
(NA)
, and
(BI)
for the reason stated in Remark
5.2
.

6.1.2.

We have

(6.1)

\displaystyle\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}))=-2.

Hence there will be a cyclic submodule

\bbdelta_{2}(T_{\mathbf{f}},\Delta_{\emptyset})\subset\bigcap^{2}_{\mathcal{R}_{f}}\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})

Remark 6.1
.

\bbdelta_{2}(T_{\mathbf{f}},\Delta_{\emptyset})
expected to be related with rank two Kolyvagin system
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2}
(cf.
[
BSS18
, Section 5]
) related to nontrivial rank two Euler system. The existence of this rank two Euler system is conjectural (cf.
[
LZ20b
, §2]
), but the hypothesis
(Plec)
assures the existence and non-vanishing. Hence
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2}
is non trivial.

We also like to remark that, though the existence of
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2}
is conjectural, but there are some recent works towards the evidence of the higher rank Euler system. In
[
FG23
]
, the authors introduced
plectic
Stark-Heegner points for an elliptic curve
A/F
given that
\lim_{s\to 1}L(A/F,s)=2
which is related to the local image of
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm Plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2}
in a parallel weight 2 specialization. Also the
“mock plectic points”
in
[
DF25
]
, may be seen an oblique evidence for the plectic philosophy of Nekovář and Scholl, using the mock analogue of Hilbert modular forms. In the Section 3.8 of
[
DF25
]
authors provide some conjectural analogy between mock plectic points and the plectic p-adic
invariants. In
[
HM22
]
, the authors defined two variable anti-cyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-functions via Stark-Heegner points.

Proposition 6.2
.

We have

\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{0}),\;\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})\in D_{\mathrm{parf}}^{[1,2]}(\prescript{}{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}}{\mathrm{Mod}})

If
\bbdelta_{2}(T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})\neq 0
, then:

(1)

If the image of
\bbdelta_{1}(T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})
under the composite map

\res^{-}_{p}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\res_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}\colon H^{1}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(F_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(F_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))

is zero then

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}})\cong\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})

are both free
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}
-modules of rank two.

(2)

\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})
is torsion, Moreover,

\det(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})/\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}\bbdelta_{1}(T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}))=\left(\det(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}))\right)^{2}.

(3)

If in addition for each
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}
,
\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}))\neq 0
, then
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{0})=0
and the
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}
-module
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{0})
has rank two.

Proof.

The fact that the Selmer complexes are perfect and that they are concentrated in the indicated degrees follows from Proposition
3.29
.

Since
\bbdelta_{2}(T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})\neq 0
, the
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}
-module
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})
is torsion by Theorem
4.8
. Therefor
\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})\in D_{\mathrm{parf}}^{[1,2]}(\prescript{}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathrm{Mod}})
and
\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}))=-2
implie that rank of
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})
equals two. Moreover, since
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}
is a finite dimensional regular local ring, for any finitely generated
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}
-module
M
we have

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}),M)=0=\operatorname{Ext}^{3}_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}}(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}),M).

Thence,
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})
is free.

Since
\varepsilon(\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda)=-1
,

(6.2)

\res^{-}_{p}(\bbdelta_{1}(T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}))=0

follows from the comparison of the class
\bbdelta_{2}(T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta)
with the Kolyvagin system stated in Remark
6.1
. Hence by (rank 2) Kato’s reciprocity law for Hilbert modular form
we have the equality

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}})\cong\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}).

which is ensured by the non triviality of
\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2}
and the fact
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})
is free. This proves the results in (1).

Statement in (2) is direct consequences of Corollary
4.14
.

In addition, if
\res_{p}(\bbdelta_{1}(T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset}))\neq 0
, then
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{0})=0
is a consequence of conclusion (1). As
\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{0}))=-2
, we get
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}
-rank of
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{0})
is two.
∎

We also choose trivializations for
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}
(cf. discussion in §
5.1.2
)

(6.3)

\LOG_{\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}\colon H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}.

and let us put
\operatorname{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}\coloneqq\LOG_{\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}\otimes_{i_{\mathbf{f}}^{*}}{\rm id}\colon H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})\rightarrow\mathcal{R}
.

By results in Proposition
6.2
.1, we can choose an isomorphism (compatible with (
6.3
))

(6.4)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Log}^{2}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}\colon\bigcap^{2}_{\mathcal{R}_{f}}\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda),\Delta_{\emptyset})\rightarrow\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}},

such that
\operatorname{Log}^{2}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2})
is a generator of
\bbdelta_{2}(T_{\mathbf{f}},\Delta_{\emptyset})
.

6.1.3.

Let us focus on
\mathbf{T}_{2}
, we have

\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))=-2

Hence we have a cyclic submodule

\bbdelta_{2}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\subset\bigcap^{2}_{R}\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})

On the other hand, we have

\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}}))=0

Hence we have a submodule

\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}})\subset\mathcal{R}

The exact sequence (
3.3
) in Lemma
3.6
gives a map

(6.5)

\res_{/\mathrm{bal}}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}}\colon\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R}.

Let us fix trivializations for
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}
compatible with (
6.3
) (cf. §
5.1.3
)

\operatorname{EXP}^{*}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}\colon H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{f}}

and define
\operatorname{Exp}^{*}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,\mathfrak{p}}
as the following composition

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\xrightarrow{\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}}}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{EXP}^{*}_{\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}\otimes\operatorname{id}}\mathcal{R}.

Remark 6.3
.

If
\operatorname{Exp}^{*}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))
for all
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}
is not trivial, then Theorem
4.20
. tells

(6.6)

\bigotimes_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\operatorname{Exp}^{*}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))=\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}})).

We note that
\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{?})\in D_{\mathrm{parf}}^{[1,2]}(\prescript{}{\mathcal{R}}{\mathrm{Mod}})
for
?=\mathbf{g},\mathrm{bal}
and hence

(6.7)

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}})=\{0\},\quad\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}})\text{ is torsion,}

(6.8)

\begin{split}&\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\text{ is torsion free of rank two,}\quad\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\text{ is torsion,}\\
&\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{R}^{2}\text{ is injective}\end{split}

whenever
\bbdelta_{2}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\neq 0
.

6.1.4.

Note that we have

\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))=0

. Therefore we get a submodule of leading terms

(6.9)

\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\subset\mathcal{R}.

Recall also the local conditions
\Delta_{+}
, we have
\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{+}))=-2
. Hence we get submodule

\bbdelta_{2}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{+})\subset\bigcap^{2}_{R}\widetilde{H}^{1}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{+})

Let us consider the natural map
\res^{/\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
as the composition of (induced by the inclusion
\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}^{+}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3}
) for
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}

\widetilde{H}^{1}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{+})\rightarrow H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\otimes_{O}\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{O}\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}(\lambda))\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R},

Then define

\res^{/\mathbf{g}}_{p}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\res^{/\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}.

Lets assume that
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq 0

Remark 6.4
.

Theorem
4.20
gives us

\bigotimes_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\operatorname{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda)}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{+}))=\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{+})).

We further remark that
\widetilde{H}_{\rm f}^{1}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=0
by Theorem
4.11
.

Proposition 6.5
.

If
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3}.\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq 0
, then also

\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\neq 0\neq\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}).

Proof.

We have the natural map

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})

is injective via
3.7
. Therefore by Remark
6.4
we get
\widetilde{H}_{\rm f}^{1}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})=0
. The self-duality of Selmer complexes gives
\widetilde{H}_{\rm f}^{2}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset})
is torsion. Hence Theorem
4.15
tells us that
\bbdelta_{2}(\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset})\neq 0
. Moreover, since

\{0\}=\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})=\ker\left(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset})\xrightarrow{\res_{p}}\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})\right),

we conclude that
\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\neq 0
as required.

We note that
3.7
and the discussion above gives rise to an exact sequence on
\mathcal{R}
-modules

(6.10)

0\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\underbrace{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})}_{\rm rank=2}\rightarrow\underbrace{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})}_{\rm torsion}\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow 0.

Hence
6.10
shows that
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is of rank two and
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is torsion. therefore we conclude that
\bbdelta_{2}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq 0
, which gives the second asserted non vanishing.
∎

We may also consider the natural map
\res^{/\mathrm{bal}}_{p}\coloneqq\res^{/\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
as the composition of (induced by the inclusion
\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3}\rightarrow\mathscr{F}^{+\mathrm{bal}^{+}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{3},\>\mathfrak{p}\mid p
)

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{+})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}\otimes_{O}\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{O}\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}(\lambda))\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathcal{R}

Remark 6.6
.

\operatorname{Exp}^{*}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{+}))=0
and we also get that
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}})=0
.

6.2.
Factorization

We will explicitly describe the map

\delta^{1}\colon\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})

arising from
3.7
.

Let

(6.11)

x_{\mathrm{f}}\coloneqq(x,(x_{v}),(\xi_{v}))\in\widetilde{Z}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}\left(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}\right)

be any cocycle:

x\in Z^{1}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2}),\>x_{v}\in U_{v}(\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{T}_{2}),\>\xi_{v}\in\mathbf{T}_{2},

dx_{v}=0,\quad\res_{v}(x)-i^{+}_{v}(x_{v})=d\xi_{v}\quad\forall v\in\Sigma.

Since
U^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{T}_{2})\coloneqq C^{\bullet}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{T}_{2})
and
F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{T}_{2}
is isomorphic image of
F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{T}_{3}
, we have

U^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{T}_{2})\cong U^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\Delta_{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{T}_{3})

for all
\mathfrak{p}\mid p
. Hence we can view
x_{\mathfrak{p}}=:y_{\mathfrak{p}}
as an element of
U^{1}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\Delta_{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{T}_{3})
for all
\mathfrak{p}\mid p
.
For each
v\in\Sigma\backslash\mathbbl{P}
, we have a canonical identification

U^{1}_{v}(\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{T}_{2})\oplus U^{1}_{v}(\Delta_{0},\mathbf{T}_{1})\cong U^{1}_{v}(\Delta_{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{T}_{3})

which arises from the split short exact sequence
2.12
. We also have the following identification

C^{\bullet}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2})\oplus C^{\bullet}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1})\cong C^{\bullet}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3})

Put
y=\iota(x)\;+\tr(0)
, which is a cocycle in
Z^{1}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3})
with
dy=0
. Similarly we can define

\mu_{v}=\iota(\xi_{v})+\tr(0)\in\mathbf{T}_{3}\quad\forall v\in\Sigma.

Then the cochain

y_{\mathrm{f}}\coloneqq(y,(y_{v}),(\mu_{v}))\in\widetilde{C}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}\left(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}\right)

maps to
x_{\mathbf{f}}
under the morphism
\pi_{\tr}
since we have
\pi_{\tr}\circ\iota=\operatorname{id}_{\mathbf{T}_{2}}
. Moreover, for all
v\in\Sigma^{p}
, we have

d\mu_{v}-\res_{v}(y)+i_{v}^{+}(y_{v})=\iota(d\xi_{v}-\res_{v}(x)+i_{v}^{+}(x_{v}))=0.

Now let’s focus on
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbbl{P}
. Observe that the image of
d\mu_{\mathfrak{p}}-\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(y)+i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(y_{\mathfrak{p}})\in C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{3})
under the morphism

C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{3})\xrightarrow{\pi_{\tr}}C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{2})

equals
d\xi_{\mathfrak{p}}-\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(x)+i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(x_{\mathfrak{p}})=0
, hence

\begin{split}d\mu_{\mathfrak{p}}-\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(y)+i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(y_{\mathfrak{p}})&\in\ker\left(C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{3})\xrightarrow{\pi_{\tr}}C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{2})\right)\\
&=\operatorname{im}\left(C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})\xhookrightarrow{\tr}C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{3})\right).\end{split}

If we denote
z_{\mathfrak{p}}\in C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})
such that

\tr(z_{\mathfrak{p}})=d\mu_{\mathfrak{p}}-\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(y)+i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(y_{\mathfrak{p}}),

we get

dy_{\mathrm{f}}=\left(dy=0,(dx_{v}=0)_{v\in\Sigma},(\{0\}_{v\in\Sigma\backslash\mathbbl{P}},\{\tr z_{\mathfrak{p}}\}_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbbl{P}})\right)\in\widetilde{C}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}\left(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}\right)

As
d\mu_{\mathfrak{p}}-\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(y)+i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(y_{\mathfrak{p}})\in C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{3})
is a cocycle, we get
d\tr(z_{\mathfrak{p}})=0
and so
z_{\mathfrak{p}}\in C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})
is also a cocycle. We conclude that

\tr(0,0,z_{p})_{\mathrm{f}}=(0,(0)_{v\in\Sigma},(\{0\}_{v\in\Sigma\backslash\mathbbl{P}},\{\tr z_{\mathfrak{p}}\}_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbbl{P}}))\in\widetilde{Z}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}\left(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\tr\Delta_{0}\right)

and
\tr(0,0,z_{p})_{\mathrm{f}}=dy_{\mathrm{f}}
, where
(0,0,z_{p})_{\mathrm{f}}\coloneqq(0,(0)_{v\in\Sigma},(\{0\}_{v\in\Sigma\backslash\mathbbl{P}},\{z_{\mathfrak{p}}\}_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbbl{P}}))
. Hence we get the following proposition:

Proposition 6.7
.

For a cocycle given as in (
6.11
), we have

\delta^{1}([x_{\mathrm{f}}])=[(0,0,z_{p})_{\mathrm{f}}]\in\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})

Remark 6.8
.

We note that for
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}
the diagram

\textstyle{\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{T}_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\tr}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{T}_{3}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+\mathbf{g}}\mathbf{T}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}

does not commute. In consequence,
z_{\mathfrak{p}}
comes about to account for this failure.

6.2.1.

Now we will describe the cocycle
z_{\mathfrak{p}}
in terms of
y_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Let
\{u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\}\subset\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}}
denote an
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
-basis of and let
\{u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}},u^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\}\subset T_{\mathbf{g}}
be an extended basis. Let us denote by
\{\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}},\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\}\subset T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}
the dual basis. Recall that

\mathrm{Fil}^{2}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\coloneqq\operatorname{Hom}(T_{\mathbf{g}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)=\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}}\widehat{\otimes}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}(\lambda)=\left\langle u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}},u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right\rangle

\begin{split}\mathrm{Fil}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\coloneqq&\ker\{\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}},F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}})\}(\lambda)\\
=&\left\langle u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}},u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}-u^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right\rangle\end{split}

\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2}=T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{Fil}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)

\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathbf{g}}_{v}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)\coloneqq\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}},F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)=\left\langle u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right\rangle\subset\mathrm{Fil}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)

all are
G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-stable submodule such that the
G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
action on the quotient

\mathrm{gr}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\coloneqq\mathrm{Fil}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)/\mathrm{Fil}^{\mathbf{g}}_{v}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda)

is via
\lambda
(c.f. the proof of Lemma
3.6
).

Let us fix a basis
\{w_{1},w_{2}\}
of
T_{\mathbf{f}}
. Given
g\in G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, let us define
a_{i}(g),b_{i}(g)\in\mathcal{R}
(
i=1,2
) such that

i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(x_{\mathfrak{p}})(g)=\sum_{i=1}^{2}a_{i}(g)(u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}})\otimes w_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}b_{i}(g)(u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}-u^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}})\otimes w_{i}.

Hence the cocycle
i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(y_{\mathfrak{p}})\in C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathbf{T}_{3}})
is then given explicitly by

i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(y_{\mathfrak{p}})(g)=\sum_{i=1}^{2}a_{i}(g)(u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}})\otimes w_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}b_{i}(g)(u^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\breve{u}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}})\otimes w_{i}.

By definition, the cocycle
\operatorname{tr}\circ i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(y_{\mathfrak{p}})\in C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})
is the cocycle

\operatorname{tr}\circ i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(y_{\mathfrak{p}})(g)=\sum_{i=1}^{2}b_{i}(g)w_{i}.

Hence we get

Proposition 6.9
.

The cocycle
z_{\mathfrak{p}}=\operatorname{tr}\circ i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(y_{\mathfrak{p}})
agrees with the image of
i_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}(x_{\mathfrak{p}})
under the natural projection map

(6.12)

C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{2})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}_{/\mathbf{g},\mathfrak{p}}}C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},T_{\mathbf{f}}\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{gr}^{+\mathbf{g}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\operatorname{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})(\lambda))\xrightarrow[\rm Lemma\ \ref{localses}]{\sim}C^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},T_{\mathbf{f}}(\lambda))\otimes\mathcal{R},

In particular the morphism

factors as

.

Suppose

. then the map

in our present set-up, we have the following exact sequence for all

0\rightarrow\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}\rightarrow\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}\rightarrow H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})\rightarrow 0

is also torsion-free.

Proposition 6.11
.

In the setting of Corollary
6.10
, we have

(6.18)

\times\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset}))

Proof.

We have exact sequence

(6.19)

0\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset})\xrightarrow{\res_{p}}\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})\xrightarrow{\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}}\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset})\rightarrow 0.

Hence we get another exact sequence

(6.20)

0\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\dfrac{\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}}{\res_{/\mathrm{Pan},\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\xrightarrow{\partial_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}^{1}}\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})}{\delta^{1}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset})\rightarrow 0.

Therefore we have

(6.21)

\Char\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})}{\delta^{1}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)\\
=\Char\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\dfrac{\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}}{\res_{/\mathrm{Pan},\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\\
=\Char\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}\right)\Char\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)\\
\times\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset}))

∎

Theorem 6.12
.

Suppose
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3}.\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq 0
and the restriction
\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\neq 0
for all
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{1}
, and
\mathfrak{p}_{2}
. Then we get

\Char\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\\
=\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}}))\left\{\Char\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}\right)\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\right\}.

Proof.

From (
3.7
), we get an exact sequence

(6.22)

0\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\xrightarrow{\delta^{1}}\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow 0

This shows that

(6.23)

\Char\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})}{\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})}\right)\Char\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\\
=\Char\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})}{\delta^{1}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)\Char\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)

Hence we get

(6.24)

\Char\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\Char\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)=\Char\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})}{\delta^{1}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)\\
=\Char\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{-}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{/\mathrm{bal},\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)\Char\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}\right)\\
\hfill\times\quad\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\qquad\qquad\\
=\left\{\bigotimes_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\operatorname{Exp}^{*}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,\mathfrak{p}}(\bbdelta_{1}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\tr\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\right\}\Char\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}\right)\\
\hfill\times\quad\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset}))

By Remark
6.3
we get

(6.25)

\Char\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\\
=\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}}))\left\{\Char\left(\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}\right)\Char(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\right\}

∎

Corollary 6.13
.

If
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{\mathcal{R}\rm-torsion}
is a pseudo-null then we have

(6.26)

\displaystyle\bbdelta_{0}(T_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}})i_{\mathbf{f}}^{*}\left(\operatorname{Log}^{2}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2})\right).

Proof.

From the exact triangle

(6.27)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{0})\rightarrow\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}R\Gamma(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{+}\mathbf{T}_{1})\xrightarrow{+1}

we get that

\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{H^{1}(G_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathscr{F}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{T}_{1})}{\res_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}))}\cong\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{f}}(G_{F,\Sigma},\mathbf{T}_{1},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{\mathcal{R}\rm-torsion}.

Hence from the given condition and
Theorem

6.12
, the required result follows immediately.
∎

Remark 6.14
.

As previously noted, we expect that (in view of Iwasawa–Greenberg main conjectures)
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{3},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is generated by
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\operatorname{As}(\mathbf{g}))(P,Q)
and
\bbdelta_{0}(\mathbf{T}_{2},\operatorname{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}})
is generated by
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\mathrm{bal}}(\mathbf{f}\otimes\breve{\mathbf{g}})(P,Q)
. Whereas
\operatorname{Log}^{2}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda}(\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,1}\wedge\mathbf{BK}^{\rm plec}_{\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda,2})
is expected to interpolate the second order derivatives of Perrin-Riou’s
\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(V_{f\otimes\lambda})\otimes\cal{H}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{cycl}})
-valued
p
-adic
L
-function of
f\otimes\lambda
, a crystalline specialization of
\mathbf{f}\otimes\lambda
(cf.
[
Per93
,
Per00
]
).

We also want to remark that this situation is a real quadratic version of the case stated in
[
DR16
, §4.5.3]
.
